### Dispatch M4, independent read of map 5 `republic-of-korea` and of the `tux` re-crop (2026-11-21, `Shadow`)

Artifacts I read and measured, all under `/tmp/opencode/t0042`. Map 5: `republic-of-korea.scm`, `republic-of-korea.chk`, `republic-of-korea_report.json`, `korea-100c.png`, `korea-reopen.png`, `korea-100.png`, `korea-100b.png`, `korea-jump1.png`, `korea-mm.png`, `korea-mm-2x.png`, `korea-booth64.png`, `korea-booth32.png`, `republic-of-korea-mm.png`. `tux` re-crop: `tux-mm.png`, `tux-booth64.png`, `tux-booth32.png`, `tux-100.png`, `tux-reopen.png`, `tux-jump.png`, `tux.scm`, `tux_report.json`. The upstream mask source I re-fetched myself to `/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042/m4-KOR.geo.json` with `curl` from `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/johan/world.geo.json/master/countries/KOR.geo.json`. `korea.py` and `tux.py` were read for their recorded parameters and not executed. Files I listed but did not measure: `korea-map-body.png`, `korea-map-body-2x.png`, `korea-map-body-256.png`, `korea-thumb-crop.png`, `korea-thumb-crop-2x.png`, `korea-thumb-b-2x.png`, `korea-thumb-2x.png`, `korea-left-side.png`, `korea-left-100b.png`, `korea-title.png`, `korea-status.png`, `korea-status-crop.png`, `korea-status-full.png`, `korea-profile.png`, `korea-p2.png`, `korea-preview.png`, `korea-preview-2x.png`, `tux-left-100.png`, `tux-title.png`, `tux-thumb-check-2x.png`, `tux-jump2.png`, `tux-jump3.png`. The synthetic `republic-of-korea-calibration-render.png` and `-4x.png` are named and excluded from contrast evidence, per the booth-artifact decision at doc:92. **The `tux` belly was not read, not measured and not graded**, per the `## Status` decision of 2026-11-20 21:10.

Method. Four scripts I wrote for this read, `/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042/m4_korea.py`, `m4_korea2.py`, `m4_korea3.py`, `m4_korea4.py`, reproducible with `python3` against `/tmp/opencode/t0042`. They import nothing from the builder: no `korea.py`, `rocky.py`, `build_ts.py`, `measure_*.py`. The map mask ground truth is my own parse of the `MTXM` section of `staredit\scenario.chk` extracted from the published `.scm` with `/tmp/opencode/t0042/chkdump` (StormLib, MIT, `ladislav/StormLib` as pinned at doc:234): extracted 614215 B, head `56455220020000003b00495645520200`, **byte-identical to the published `republic-of-korea.chk`**. My own `sha256sum` of the controller `republic-of-korea.scm` is `50e55299dd86fafbcf5976f841992dd326e3d9b56c4d27fecbfdebfd06ea03ca`, equal to the recorded value. My independent geography control is my own scanline even-odd rasterization of the upstream ring I fetched. No target contact of any kind.

**Two verdicts, stated plainly, before the findings.**

1. **Map 5 `republic-of-korea` FAILS the legibility gate as written, at every booth scale I read, on unaided identifiability.** The mask is correct, the projection is correct, the geometry reproduces the upstream outline at 95.86 percent IoU, contrast passes at 6.745, and re-open is pixel-identical. What fails is the thing the gate actually measures. At the real 128x128 overview render I read a coarse landmass with a pointed north-east corner, a stepped west coast, a broad middle and a tapering tail, plus one small separate body below the west end. I could confirm each of those against the real geography after I already knew the answer. I could not read it as South Korea from shape alone. At 64 px it reads as a dark blob with a dot under it. At 32 px it reads as nothing. The map is identifiable **only with the name supplied**, and the captured title bar shows no name, see finding 6.
2. **The `tux` re-crop `M3` carried is confirmed correct, and my `M2` should-fix at doc:2310 is closed.** `tux-mm.png` is byte-identical to the crop (18,112)-(146,241) of both `tux-100.png` and `tux-reopen.png`, it carries the whole silhouette including the feet, and `tux-booth64.png` and `tux-booth32.png` are exact Lanczos downscales of it. My 64 px read of Tux is unchanged and still passes. My 32 px read is unchanged and still fails, and the re-crop could not have changed that, because it is a crop fix and not a shape fix.

### Map 5 does not read as South Korea at booth scale without the name, and the reason is the resolution of the source outline, not the pipeline
**Severity:** blocker
**Where:** `korea-mm.png`, `korea-booth64.png`, `korea-booth32.png`, `korea-100c.png` overview render at (18,112)-(146,240), `korea.py` mask source, doc:1894 Option B
**What I read.** The 128x128 map body of `korea-100c.png` enlarged to 384 px (`/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042/m4-korea-body-3x.png`). My own correct 64 px and 32 px downscales of that body (`m4-korea-booth64-correct.png`, `m4-korea-booth32-correct.png`, enlarged with nearest neighbour for reading). The published `korea-booth64.png` and `korea-booth32.png`. A side-by-side of the map mask against my own independent rasterization of the upstream ring (`m4-korea-mask-vs-geo.png`), which are visually indistinguishable, consistent with the 95.86 percent IoU.
**What I see.** At 128 px, a filled mass whose northern boundary rises from a shoulder in the west to a point just east of centre and then falls steeply to the east, an indented west coast with two steps, a smooth rounded east side, a south end tapering to a blunt tail, and one detached small ellipse below the west end. Nothing in that description is uniquely Korean. The features that make Korea recognizable at a glance, the deeply indented south-west coast with its bays, the south-east bulge around Busan, the narrow neck at Pohang, and Ulleungdo, are all absent, because the source ring has 18 vertices over a 4.22 by 3.35 degree box. At 64 px the mass is 30 x 47 px and the detached body is 7 x 4 px. At 32 px the mass is 15 x 23 px and the detached body is 3 x 2 px, and I cannot read either as a country.
**Failure scenario:** on 2026-11-28 the presenter opens `republic-of-korea.scm`, shows the overview thumbnail, and says nothing. A visitor at the booth, including a Korean visitor at a Seoul conference, looks at the silhouette on the screen and cannot say which country it is. The DoD rows at doc:171 and doc:172, each map "described by what a player sees" and its design intent carried by a technique that works, are then met only by narration, and the legibility gate box at doc:174 is not met at all, because an independent reader of the artifact cannot identify the intended picture. The other four maps pass this test, `tux` at 64 px, the heart at 32 px, the Rocky disc at 32 px, "FOSS for All" at 4x. Map 5 is the one that does not.
**Suggested direction, ranked, and the first one is cheap.** 1. Re-rasterize from a higher-resolution ring, Natural Earth 50 m or 10 m South Korea, which carries the south-west bays and the south-east coast, and gate it on the same four numbers. My measured 10-tile floor means a feature must be at least 10 tiles wide to have any chance at 64 px, and 20 tiles to read at 32 px, so the ring has to be filtered at that floor rather than simplified to 18 vertices. This is a mask data change against the same pipeline, one dispatch, seven days. 2. If that cannot be afforded, keep the current mask and record map 5's disposition honestly: the silhouette is a coarse, geographically correct outline whose identifiability depends on the name being shown, which makes finding 6 a prerequisite rather than a nicety. 3. Do not attempt to fix recognizability by adding marks inside the shape. That is the two-tone internal feature class this task already excluded at doc:84 and doc:2206.

### The booth artifacts for map 5 are one third editor panel and are aspect-distorted by 23 percent, and the crop the report calls the map body is not the map body
**Severity:** blocker
**Where:** `republic-of-korea_report.json` `overview_map_body_crop_abs` [8,85,149,259] and `booth_artifacts`, artifacts `korea-mm.png`, `korea-mm-2x.png`, `korea-booth64.png`, `korea-booth32.png`
**Problem:** the real overview map render inside the editor is at absolute **(18,112)-(146,240), 128x128 px, 0.5 px per tile**. I located it by my own largest-connected-block search over map tones (greyscale 140 to 195, or bluish glyph tones), and it is the identical rectangle in all five whole-map captures I tested, `korea-100c.png`, `korea-reopen.png`, `tux-100.png`, `tux-reopen.png`, `tux-jump.png`. The report's crop begins 10 px left and 27 px above that and runs 14 px past its right edge and 20 px past its bottom, so `korea-mm.png` (142x175) is 27.46 percent white panel chrome and 5.06 percent panel-text antialias, 32.5 percent of the artifact is not map. `korea-booth64.png` and `korea-booth32.png` are exact Lanczos downscales of that crop (0 px difference against my own resize of the same pixels), and because 142x175 to 64x64 is not a uniform scale (x 0.4507, y 0.3657), the 128x128 map render lands as **57.7 x 46.8 px** inside `booth64` and 28.8 x 23.4 px inside `booth32`. Measured composition of `booth64`: 16.24 percent glyph, 52.91 percent ground, **21.12 percent chrome**, 9.74 percent antialias mid-tone. Measured composition of `booth32`: 16.21 percent glyph, **18.16 percent chrome**, 18.07 percent mid.
**Failure scenario:** the silhouette's aspect is the identity of this map. On the map it is 121 x 187 tiles, 0.647 wide-over-tall, and my own rasterization of the upstream polygon gives 0.642, so the map is right. In `booth64` it becomes 0.797, a 23.2 percent horizontal stretch, so the gate artifact shows a visibly wider and shorter Korea than the map contains. A reader who ticks the legibility gate from `korea-booth64.png` also reads coverage 16.24 percent against the code coverage 25.01 percent and reports an 8.8-point loss of design tiles. There is no such loss: my correct 64 px downscale of the true 128x128 body measures 24.63 percent glyph and 0.02 percent chrome. Both errors come from the artifact, not from the map.
**How the wrong crop was produced, stated because the report shows the trace.** `overview_map_body_crop_panel` is recorded as [0,-20,141,154], a panel-relative box with a **negative y**, which is what a body located 27 px above the point the script assumed looks like. `overview_map_body_crop_abs` [8,85,149,259] is that same box clamped into the capture, and it is 141x174 while the artifact on disk is 142x175, so even the crop the report states does not describe the file it produced.

**What is right and I confirmed:** `korea-mm.png` is a real capture crop, byte-identical (0 differing px at threshold 16) to the crop (8,85)-(150,260) of `korea-100c.png` and of `korea-reopen.png`, and `korea-mm-2x.png` is an exact 2x Lanczos enlargement of it (0 px difference). The provenance chain capture to artifact is honest, only the crop bounds are wrong.
**Suggested direction:** re-crop from the measured (18,112)-(146,240), regenerate `korea-mm.png`, `-mm-2x`, `-booth64`, `-booth32` from that, and record the panel map-render rectangle as a measured constant, because it is the same in five captures across two maps and every future crop can now be derived instead of guessed. `tux` already shows the correct shape of this fix, see its own finding below.

### Map 5 coverage, mask contents and the recorded projection are confirmed by an independent parse and an independent rasterization
**Severity:** nit, agreement recorded
**Where:** `republic-of-korea.scm`, `republic-of-korea.chk`, `republic-of-korea_report.json` `code_coverage_pct`, `mask_bbox`, `overview_map_body_crop_abs`
**My measurement from the shipped file.** `MTXM` 131072 B, 65536 u16 entries, distinct values exactly {64, 192}, value 192 covers 16390 tiles = **25.01 percent**, `MTXM` byte-equal to `TILE`, `ISOM` 265224 B containing only 0x00 and 0x10, design bbox (78,198,11,250) exactly as recorded, two 8-connected components of 16029 and 361 tiles, **zero enclosed ground tiles** by my own border flood fill, no `UNIT` payload, `DOOD` absent, `MRGN` 1280 B. The second component is the island, bbox (78,106,235,250), 29 x 16 tiles.
**My measurement from the upstream data.** I fetched the ring myself: 19 coordinate pairs, 18 distinct vertices, closed ring, lon 126.117398 to 129.468304, lat 34.390046 to 38.612243. Rasterizing it with the projection exactly as recorded at doc:1894, latitude 38.85 N to 33.10 N over 256 rows with cosine longitude correction at 36 N, gives coverage **24.93 percent**, bbox rows 11 to 198 and width 122, against the map's 11 to 197 and 121. After a 78-tile x alignment the IoU between my mask and the map mask is **95.86 percent**, per-row left-and-right edge deviation is 1.37 tiles mean and 7 tiles worst, with zero rows deviating by more than 10 tiles. Mainland aspect 121/187 = 0.647 against the geographic 0.642. **The mask is the upstream outline, reproduced correctly, at the recorded projection.** That is the first time this task has checked a mask against its named upstream source rather than against the builder's own code, and it passes.
**Coverage on the render.** On the true 128x128 body of `korea-100c.png` I measure 4108 glyph px of 16384 = **25.07 percent**, matching the recorded 25.07. The overview render is a 2x decimation of the tile grid (0.5 px per tile), so the rendered share and the tile share agree by construction, and 4108 x 4 = 16432 tiles against the mask's 16390 is decimation rounding.

### Contrast on map 5 passes with the largest margin of any artifact in this task, and the headline figure is one tone pair rather than four measurements
**Severity:** nit, agreement recorded
**Where:** `republic-of-korea_report.json` `real_thumbnail_wcag`, `main_canvas_wcag`, `booth64_wcag`, `calibration_render_wcag`

WCAG 6.745. I reproduce 6.745 exactly and I confirm there is no tone spread to argue about on the map itself. On the main-canvas boundary frame `korea-jump1.png` crop (155,105)-(1878,832) both tones are present in quantity, 48.73 percent glyph against 43.96 percent ground, and the pair measured on the tones actually present there, (174,174,174) against (40,51,76), is **5.674**, worst ground-against-glyph **3.662**. Every figure is above the 3.0 gate. **Verdict: I agree with 6.745 on the map and I confirm the contrast gate passes on map 5.** Two qualifications stated so the row is not over-read. The report prints 6.745 four times and all four come from that one tone pair, the same residual nit I recorded at doc:2200. And on the published booth artifacts the boundary mid-tone band is 9.74 percent of `booth64` and 18.07 percent of `booth32`, because Lanczos across a tile grid manufactures intermediate tones; that is a property of downscaling any hard-edged map, not a property of this map's palette, and it is why the honest contrast number is the one measured on the map body.

### Map 5's outline carries no coastal feature at all, so the recorded 10-tile coastal floor is met by absence rather than by design
**Severity:** should-fix
**Where:** `republic-of-korea_report.json`, `korea.py`, doc:84, doc:2107, `## Next Actions` doc:210 "coastal features at 10 tiles or larger"
**Problem, measured on the mask in the published file.** Rows containing two or more separate glyph runs: **1 row out of 256**. Largest ground gap enclosed between two glyph runs in the same row: **8 tiles**. Enclosed ground tiles: 0. Narrowest glyph run in any row: 2 tiles, and that is the taper of the shape's northern point at row 11, not a coastal feature. The width profile of the mainland is 2 tiles at row 11, 62 at 31, 101 at 51, 111 at 91, 115 at 111, 108 at 131, 102 at 151, 81 at 171, 38 at 191, a broad body tapering to a point in the north-east and to a narrow tail in the south. There is no bay, no inlet, no strait and no peninsula in the silhouette, because an 18-vertex 110 m outline removes all of them from a coastline that in reality has many.
**Failure scenario:** the acceptance line recorded at doc:210 says coastal features are 10 tiles or larger. A reader takes that to mean the map shows a articulated coast, and the map in fact shows a smooth coast with no articulation, which is the single biggest reason a Korean visitor at the booth may not read the silhouette as Korea. The claim is technically true and misleading in effect, and the record should say which.
**Suggested direction:** restate the design intent as "coarse filled outline from a 110 m 18-vertex simplification, no coastal features", and if coastal articulation is wanted, it has to come from a higher-resolution ring (Natural Earth 50 m or 10 m) rather than from adding marks by hand. That is a mask data change, not a pipeline change, and it is testable in one pass against the same gate.

### The `tux` re-crop is confirmed. My `M2` should-fix at doc:2310 is closed
**Severity:** nit, closure recorded
**Where:** `tux-mm.png`, `tux-booth64.png`, `tux-booth32.png`, `tux-100.png`, `tux-reopen.png`, `tux.scm`
**Provenance, measured.** `tux-mm.png` (128x129) is **byte-identical, 0 differing pixels at threshold 8**, to the crop (18,112)-(146,241) of `tux-100.png` **and** to the same crop of `tux-reopen.png`. `tux-booth64.png` and `tux-booth32.png` are exact Lanczos downscales of `tux-mm.png`, 0 differing pixels against my own resize of the same pixels. The crop now starts 1 px up and 1 px left of the true map render I located at (18,112)-(146,240), so it carries one extra row of panel border, 0.78 percent of its pixels, which is the honest edge of a real capture and not a design defect.
**Composition, measured by me.** `tux-mm.png`: 66.55 percent glyph, 32.23 percent ground, 0.45 percent chrome, 0.78 percent mid-tone, exactly one ground tone (176,176,176) and one glyph tone (36,40,56), WCAG 6.745. That is exactly the 66.55 percent I predicted for a correctly cropped body at doc:2313. `tux-booth64.png`: 65.48 percent glyph, 0.02 percent chrome, 4.37 percent mid. `tux-booth32.png`: 65.43 percent glyph, 0 percent chrome, 4.30 percent mid. The 0.45 percent chrome inside the body is the 74 px white viewport-indicator rectangle the editor draws over the map, present at the same 74 px in every whole-map capture I tested, on both maps. It is editor overlay, not map content, and a coverage script should name it rather than count it as ground.
**Mask from the published file, my own `MTXM` parse of `tux.scm`.** Coverage 67.10 percent, design bbox (0,255,0,255), glyph present in row 0 (14 tiles), row 255 (68 tiles), column 0 (54 tiles) and column 255 (54 tiles), so the truncation note `M3` recorded is confirmed from the file. Only rows 254 and 255 contain more than one glyph run in the bottom 26 rows, so the foot separation is a 1-tile gap, which is why the feet do not survive any downscale. 70 rows contain two or more runs in total, maximum internal ground gap 42 tiles, narrowest run 8 tiles. Feature survival on the tile mask, my area-average decimation: at 128, 35 rows with two or more runs and a 21 px internal gap; at 64, 16 rows and 8 px; at 32, 7 rows and 5 px.
**My visual read, unchanged from `M2`.** At 64 px I read a round head, a shoulder step, two lateral flipper lobes separated from the body by visible notches, and a base with feet merged into it. It reads as Tux. At 32 px the head and the notches collapse and the figure reads as a mass with two side lobes. The re-crop could not change either verdict, because it fixed where the crop sat and not what the shape contains.

### Jeju Island is not in the outline the record names as the source, and the vertex count in the record is wrong
**Severity:** should-fix
**Where:** `republic-of-korea.scm` mask rows 235 to 250, doc:1894 Option B, doc:30 and doc:189 licence item
**My measurement.** The ring I fetched has 19 coordinate pairs, **18 distinct vertices**, closed, lon 126.117398 to 129.468304, lat 34.390046 to 38.612243. The record at doc:1894 calls it a "17-vertex" outline. The ring's southernmost latitude is 34.390046 N. The island in the mask occupies rows 235 to 250, which under the recorded projection is latitude 33.21 to 33.59 N, so **the island is not in the source ring**. It is a hand-added body of 361 tiles in a 29 x 16 box, which at the recorded scale is 0.81 degrees of longitude wide against Jeju's real 0.60, about a third too wide, and its 37-row gap to the mainland is roughly right.
**Failure scenario:** `## Status` doc:30 and `## Next Actions` doc:189 hand `Charmy` and `Flicky` a licence item described as "the Korea mask was rasterized from the 17-vertex South Korea outline ... and that is imported open data". As written that covers the whole mask. It does not: the island is the team's own geometry and is the only part of map 5 that is not derived open data. If the documentation repo then says the silhouette is traceable to the upstream dataset, the sentence is wrong in a way a reader can check against the upstream file, which I just did.
**Suggested direction:** record the island as a hand-placed ellipse with its dimensions and its latitude band, keep the upstream attribution for the mainland where it belongs, and correct 17 to 18 distinct vertices. This is a wording change before `Charmy` and `Flicky` rule, not after.

**Severity:** nit
**Where:** `republic-of-korea_report.json` `booth_artifacts`, `## Next Actions` doc:210, `## Status` doc:28, `/tmp/opencode/t0042/`
**Problem.** All four files the report names exist under `korea-*`, but `republic-of-korea-mm.png` is a byte-identical duplicate of `korea-mm.png` while `republic-of-korea-booth64.png`, `republic-of-korea-booth32.png` and `republic-of-korea-mm-2x.png` do not exist, although maps 2, 3 and 4 all carry the `<map>-*` form (`rocky-logo-booth64.png`, `rocky-with-heart-booth64.png`, `tux-booth64.png`). Separately, `korea-100.png` and `korea-100b.png` contain **no whole-map render** in the overview panel: my connected-block locate in the left strip finds 616 px with 0 glyph pixels in both, against 16060 px with 4108 glyph pixels in `korea-100c.png`, and the crop (8,85)-(150,260) of those two captures differs from `korea-mm.png` by 16367 px of 24850.
**Failure scenario:** `Vector` writes the map notes following the `<map>-*` convention, cites `republic-of-korea-booth64.png`, and a reader finds no such file, or finds two `-mm.png` files and cannot tell which the gate verdict was made on. A later reader treats `korea-100.png` as a second whole-map confirmation of the silhouette when it shows no map at all.

**Verdict on the gate question this read was staged to answer.** The silhouette pipeline still carries filled silhouettes, and the `tux` confirmation on the corrected crop keeps that ruling intact. What map 5 adds is a new limit that no earlier read stated. The pipeline reproduces whatever outline it is given faithfully, at 95.86 percent IoU against its own upstream source, with contrast and persistence to spare. **Fidelity is no longer the risk. Source resolution is.** A silhouette whose recognizability depends on fine coastline detail cannot be made legible by this pipeline at an 18-vertex input, and the fix is a better input ring rather than a better renderer. The `tux` belly stays out of scope and I did not read it. The GR-graphics contingency at doc:1445 stays closed on this evidence, because nothing here points at the renderer.

Artifacts I read and measured, all under `/tmp/opencode/t0042`. My geography control is my own 4x4 point-sampled even-odd scanline rasterization of the 53 rings of the `ISO_A2 == "KR"` feature, at the projection recorded at doc:2009. **Three verdicts, stated before the findings.**

1. **`republic-of-korea` as rebuilt PASSES the identifiability gate at the real 128x128 whole-map overview render, and passes at 64 px with a stated bound. It is booth-accepted.** It still does not read at 32 px, which is the out-of-scope bound recorded at doc:62 for `tux` and which I now record for this map on my own numbers below. ### The rebuilt map reads as Korea at 128 px from shape alone, and at 64 px as a peninsula with an island, which is the gate the `M4` build failed
**Severity:** nit, verdict recorded
**Where:** `republic-of-korea-mm.png`, `republic-of-korea-booth64.png`, `republic-of-korea-booth32.png`, `m6-read-mm-3x.png`, `m6-read-booth64-4x.png`, `m6-64px-old-vs-new.png` under `/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042`
**What I read first, before measuring.** The 128x128 body enlarged 3x with nearest neighbour. A tall landmass, broad across the north, a ragged upper edge with a peak just right of centre, the north side falling steeply and smoothly to the east. The **west coast carries a deep inlet complex at mid-west** with two distinct notches and stepped land between them. The east coast is convex and mostly smooth with one notch at mid-east. The **south coast is serrated**, a fringe of small land pieces and gaps rather than a line. One detached elongated island sits below the south-west, wider than tall. A white rectangle in the top-left corner, which is the editor viewport indicator and not map content. The features that make Korea recognizable at a glance, the indented south-west coast, the articulated south coast, the detached island, and the smooth east side against the indented west, are present. I read it as the Korean peninsula from shape alone.
**What I see at 64 px.** My dark-pixel census of the published `booth64` gives exactly **two** connected bodies: the mass at 994 px in a 32x48 px box, and the island at 21 px in a 7x5 px box. 11 rows carry two or more runs, 69 runs in total. The peninsula-plus-island-off-the-south-west gestalt survives, the west inlet is visible as a notch, and the south fringe survives as texture. At 128 px the same census gives 4 bodies, mass 3873 px in 62x96, island 74 px in 14x8, the eastern island 16 px in 5x7, 36 multi-run rows, 184 runs.
**Measured comparison against the build it replaces, my own 64 px renders from the same measured body rectangle.** New mass 32x48 px with 11 multi-run rows. Old mass 30x47 px, and my mask decimation gives **13 rows with two or more runs at 64 px on the new mask against 0 on the old**, 34 against 1 at 128 px, 0 against 0 at 32 px. At 32 px the new render is 15x23 px with a 3x2 px dot, 1 multi-run row. That is the recorded out-of-scope bound, and I record it for this map rather than leaving it inherited from `tux`.
**Honest limit on this verdict.** I knew the intended subject before I opened the images, and I cannot un-know it, so this is not a blind test. What I did instead was name the features that carry the identification and check that they are geography rather than artefact. My own independent sea-anchor test, 14 anchors of my own choosing at my own approximate coordinates with an 11x11 tile window (about 12.5 km half-width, which absorbs my own coordinate error of a few km): **9 of 14 gain sea tiles, 5 of them by 15 or more** (Incheon 0 to 52, Ganghwa Strait 0 to 35, Gangjin Bay 0 to 23, Gwangyang Bay 0 to 47, Namhae Strait 44 to 62 tiles), the others by smaller amounts (Seosanman head 11 to 16, Geum estuary 0 to 4, Pohang neck 22 to 23, Busan coast 75 to 78), **3 lose it** (Asan Bay 10 to 4, Mokpo 58 to 39, Donghae inlet 50 to 44), and 2 hold, one of which is Jinhae at 0 sea tiles in both masks, where my own anchor coordinate lands on land in both and so tests nothing, and the other is Ulleungdo's position at 110 of 121 tiles sea in both, which matches the recorded absence. The bays are real. **Residual risk stated plainly:** a visitor with no priming may read the 64 px artifact as "a peninsula with an island" rather than as Korea specifically. The 128 px overview render is where the coast carries it, so the booth should present the overview thumbnail, not only a 64 px artifact.
**Suggested direction:** none for the map. For the booth script, present this map at the overview thumbnail, which `M5` measures at 0.5 px per tile, and not at a 64 px artifact, because the difference in this map's identifiability is carried by features between 1 and 4 px at 64 px.

### The recorded 10-tile floor is not reproducible from the record as written, and the shipped mask only reproduces under a minimum-dimension reading
**Severity:** should-fix
**Where:** doc:2009 "Then the recorded 10-tile component floor drops 28 components totalling 210 tiles", doc:106 "coastal features kept at roughly 10 tiles or larger", `republic-of-korea_report.json` `new_mask.components_8conn`, `m5_raster.py` `apply_feature_floor`
**My measurement.** I rasterized the upstream 10 m feature myself: 1 feature, MultiPolygon, **53 rings, 3060 coordinate pairs, 3007 distinct vertices**, all rings closed, bbox lon 124.613617 to 131.862522 and lat 33.197577 to 38.624335, largest ring 1593 pairs. My 4x4 point-sampled even-odd fill at the recorded projection gives **15928 tiles, 24.30 percent, 34 components**. IoU against the shipped mask **0.9454**. So the shipped mask is the upstream geometry at the recorded projection, confirmed without the builder's code, the same kind of check I ran on the 110 m build at doc:2507. The floor is where the record becomes unreproducible. Applying the floor as **area of 10 tiles or more** keeps **9 components, 15825 tiles** (it keeps Ulleungdo, which I rasterize at 12 tiles in a 4x4 box at cols 247-250 rows 58-61). Applying it as **minimum dimension 10 tiles or more** keeps exactly **3 components, 15701 tiles**, which is the shipped component census `15467 + 296 + 68`, at IoU **0.9517**. My drop count under the dimension reading is **31 components, 227 tiles**, against the recorded 28 components and 210 tiles.
**Failure scenario:** a reader who re-runs this pipeline from the record, reading "10 tiles or larger" as area, ships 9 components instead of 3, including Ulleungdo, and the map they ship is not the map this read certified. `Vector` cannot reproduce the shipped mask from the documentation as written.
**Suggested direction:** name the rule as a minimum-dimension floor in `korea_10m.apply_feature_floor` and in the report, restate the dropped count under that rule, and keep Ulleungdo's absence stated the way `M5` already states it, as a consequence of the floor rather than an omission. The 3-component, 227-tile difference between my fill and the builder's fill is expected from a different sampling rule and does not need reconciling, but the floor definition does.

### The sea-anchor table carries one mislocated anchor and four land-expected anchors that sit in open water, so half of that test proves nothing
**Severity:** should-fix
**Where:** `republic-of-korea_report.json` `new_vs_old.anchors`, 22 entries with `lon`, `lat`, `col`, `row`, `expected`, `old_sea_tiles_5x5`, `new_sea_tiles_5x5`, doc:2030
**What I checked.** The table is reproducible, which is good, and its prose claim is arithmetically right: of the 12 entries marked `expected: sea`, exactly 7 gain sea, Incheon 0 to 10, Ganghwa Strait 0 to 7, Seosanman head 0 to 3, Geum estuary 0 to 3, Gwangyang Bay inner 0 to 12, Jinhae Bay inner 0 to 9, Donghae inlet 4 to 8. My own independent anchor set, different coordinates and a wider window, gives the same direction, 9 of 14 anchors gaining sea and 5 of them substantially, which is why I am not disputing the result. What I am disputing is two entries. **"Jinhae Bay inner" is recorded at lon 128.62, lat 35.16**, and Jinhae Bay is at about lon 127.55, lat 35.15, so that anchor is roughly 107 km east of the bay it names, at col 168 row 164. And **four entries marked `expected: land` sit in open water in both masks**: Gangneung coast 25 of 25 sea, NE point near Seokjin 25 of 25, SW corner near Jindo 22 of 25, Taean peninsula 7 of 25. An anchor whose whole window is sea in the map being tested cannot detect land drawn where sea should be, which is the failure the land-expected half of the table exists to catch.
**Failure scenario:** the documentation states that the rebuild restored Jinhae Bay. A reader who checks Jinhae at its real coordinates finds the test was run 107 km away, and the credibility of the other seven anchors goes with it. Separately, a reader who counts the land-expected anchors as a check on false land reads eight anchors as evidence when four of them test nothing.
**Suggested direction:** correct the Jinhae longitude, move the four land-expected anchors onto land, and re-emit the table. The claim that survives is the sea-anchor half, and it survives my independent version of it.

### Two wording nits in the `M5` record that a reader would reproduce wrongly
**Severity:** nit
**Where:** doc:2030 landmarks, `republic-of-korea_report.json` `new_mask.runs` and `old_mask.runs`
**Problem.** First, doc:2030 records "Jeju 29 tiles wide becoming 25 tiles". My measurement of the shipped mask gives the Jeju component bbox **29 columns wide** (x79-107, 17 rows tall, 296 tiles) and its **widest single run 25 tiles**. Both numbers are right and they describe different things, so the sentence reads as if the island shrank in width when it shrank in its widest run. Second, the report's `runs` field is 277 for the new mask and 2 for the old one. My count of all glyph runs over occupied rows is 405 for the new mask and 204 for the old, over 209 and 203 occupied rows. 277 and 2 are exactly the runs counted **only in rows that already have two or more runs**, which I verified by computing both readings. The key name does not say that.
**Failure scenario:** a reader recomputing the articulation claim counts runs over all rows, gets 405, and reports a 128-run discrepancy against the report, or reads 277 against my 405 as a defect in the mask rather than in the definition.
**Suggested direction:** state the denominators, "runs within multi-run rows" and "Jeju widest run 25 tiles, bbox 29 wide".

### Dispatch M8, `i-love-korea`, independent read (`Shadow`, 2026-11-21)

**What this read ran on.** My own scripts `/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042/m8_mask.py`, `m8_render.py`, `m8_legibility.py` and `m8_fixsim.py`, outputs `m8_mask.json`, `m8_render.json`, `m8_legibility.json`, `m8_fixsim.json`. No builder module is imported by any of them, and no builder measurement is quoted as evidence for a number below. `chkdump` (StormLib, MIT) is used for one thing, decompressing `i-love-korea.scm`; the chunk walk, the `MTXM` decode, the mask, the coverage, the geometry, the tone census, the contrast, the decimation-rule test and the stroke sweep are my code. Inputs are the shipped `i-love-korea.scm`, `i-love-korea.chk`, `i-love-korea-mm.png`, `i-love-korea-booth64.png`, `i-love-korea-booth32.png`, `m7-open.png`, `m7-reopen.png`, `m7-jump.png`. No target contact.

**What I saw before I measured, stated as seeing.** At the 128x128 body crop I read a serifed capital `I`, a solid heart, then five letter-shaped forms, each one a tall column of dark vertical strokes with lighter interiors. At 64 px the `I` and the heart stay dark and solid, and after the heart the row collapses into a comb of alternating dark stripes on grey with character-sized separations, the thin strokes visibly lighter than the heart and none of the closed shapes closed. At 32 px it is a bar, a heart, and a striped block. That description came from reading the three published images before any script ran.

**Criterion I set before measuring, and it is mine.** Ground tone is the dominant tone of the map body I located, [176,176,176]. A pixel is INK when its WCAG contrast against that ground tone is 3.0 or better, which is this task's own gate value, FAINT between 1.5 and 3.0, and OFF below 1.5. An element reads when INK pixels appear on at least 80 percent of its vertical extent and the INK area is at least 50 percent of the area its own `MTXM` footprint implies at that resolution. The 80 and 50 percent shapes match `M7`'s so the two reads are comparable, and the ink definition differs on purpose, contrast against ground rather than a tone classifier.

**The pipeline numbers I reproduce independently.** `chkdump` extraction 614210 B, SHA-256 `5dda6941018958c854ee4aee7426f28e4a65e0d7505c6aa87270d53272f153c0`, byte-identical to the published `i-love-korea.chk`. My own chunk walk finds `VER `, `IVER`, `VCOD`, `IOWN`, `OWNR`, `ERA `, `DIM `, `SIDE`, `MTXM` 131072 B, `PUNI`, `UPGR`, `PTEC`, `UNIT` 0 B, `ISOM` 265224 B, `TILE` 131072 B, `STR `, `MRGN` 1280 B, no `DOOD`. `MTXM` low bytes give 65536 tiles, distinct values exactly {64, 192}, value 192 covers 19666 tiles, coverage **30.01 percent**, my own perimeter 5082 edges, matching the reported figure. Mask bbox rows 9 to 246 and columns 8 to 247, so glyph height **238 tiles** with margins 9 rows top and bottom and 8 columns left and right. My own column segmentation gives seven elements, widths 15, 70, 25, 25, 25, 25, 25 tiles, gaps 5 tiles each, and the thinnest horizontal glyph run inside every letter is **5 tiles** while the thinnest vertical run is 34. Element tile counts, mine, `I` 1870, heart 3516, `K` 2380, `O` 2720, `R` 3060, `E` 3060, `A` 3060. Element glyph tiles divided by element widths give a font cell of **5 tiles wide by 34 tall**, and the row spends 48 cells: 3 for the `I`, 14 for the heart, 5 for each letter, 1 gap each. Those cell figures come out of the shipped mask, not out of `glyph.py`.

### The row does not read at the certified 64 px bound as shipped, and three of seven letterforms fall below this task's own contrast gate
**Severity:** blocker
**Where:** `/tmp/opencode/t0042/i-love-korea-booth64.png`, per-element census in `/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042/m8_legibility.json` `booth64_elements`
**Problem.** At the 64 px thumbnail-class artifact this task certifies, `I`, `O` and `E` do not hold 3.0 contrast against the ground tone over their own extent, and at 128 px all seven do, so the map is fine in the editor and the failure is specific to the 4x artifact class.
**What I measured.** Whole-artifact census against ground: 865 px at 3.0 or better (21.12 percent), 565 px between 1.5 and 3.0 (13.79 percent), 2666 px below 1.5 (65.09 percent, which is the ground itself), darkest pixel in the artifact 8.832 against ground. Per element, ink area as a percentage of the element's own mask footprint and ink rows as a percentage of its visible extent: `I` 53.0 percent and 16 of 60 rows (26.7 percent), heart 96.5 percent and 16 of 18 (88.9), `K` 67.9 and 59 of 60 (98.3), `O` 29.4 and 17 of 60 (28.3), `R` 82.6 and 59 of 60 (98.3), `E` 60.7 and 28 of 60 (46.7), `A` 86.8 and 59 of 60 (98.3). Four read, three do not, reached with a classifier that shares nothing with `M7`'s and landing on the same three letters. On the 128 px body crop the same criterion gives every element 88.6 to 100.3 percent of its implied area as INK with INK on 118 or 119 of its own extent rows, that is 100 percent row coverage for all seven, and the crop carries exactly three colours, ground 11764 px, glyph [36,40,56] 4546 px, white 74 px, with the glyph-to-ground pair at **WCAG 6.745** by my own luminance calculation.
**Failure scenario:** `S1` lists `i-love-korea` as an accepted map because it passed coverage, contrast and re-open, and the booth shows the 64 px thumbnail where `O` holds 29.4 percent of its own ink and covers a quarter of its height. A visitor sees `I`, a heart, and a striped block, and the map that was added on the operator's own instruction is the one that fails to say the thing it was built to say.
**Suggested direction:** rebuild at a stroke width the measurement says survives, see the finding on the stroke sweep below. This map is not blocked, it is unaccepted as shipped.

### SCMDraft's overview thumbnail is a top-left point sample, proven at 16384 of 16384 px, and that is the mechanism that decides every map design in this task
**Severity:** should-fix, travels to `S1`, `V1` and every later map build
**Where:** `/tmp/opencode/t0042/i-love-korea-mm.png` against my own mask decode, test and result in `/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042/m8_fixsim.json` `decimation_rule_matches_of_16310`
**What I measured.** The 128x128 body is a hard two-tone image, three colours and no blends, so the editor does not area-average when it decimates 256 tiles to 128 px. I tested candidate rules against the real crop. Sampling the **top-left tile of each 2x2 block** matches 16384 of 16384 non-overlay pixels. The next best candidate, bottom-left tile, matches 16096; `all_of_4` 16032; majority-of-four 15316; any-of-four 15289. Applying the winning rule to my own mask reproduces the published crop with **0 px differing**, and it predicts 4554 glyph px against the 4546 the crop contains, the 8 px gap being exactly the overlay in the finding below. This closes the question `M7` named at doc:2096 as a check that did not run, whether the thumbnail decimation is nearest or majority sampling.
**Failure scenario:** a designer reads coverage 30.01 percent and assumes a 5-tile stroke is 1.25 px of a 64 px artifact and will average to a visible mid-tone. It does not. The thumbnail first throws away half the tiles by point sampling, so a 5-tile stroke enters the 128 px render as a 2.5 px band whose position depends on tile parity, and the Lanczos step after that has nothing solid to blend. Any feature under 2 tiles is absent from the editor thumbnail outright, and this task has never stated that number as a design floor.
**Suggested direction:** record the sampling rule as a measured constant beside the panel rectangle `M6` recorded, and state the design floor it implies, 6 tiles for a stroke at the 64 px bound, in the brief of any map dispatch that has letterforms or thin features in it.

### The stroke measurement points at a single row at 6-tile strokes with the heart capped at 6 cells, not at fewer characters and not first at a two-line layout
**Severity:** should-fix, this is the fix direction the numbers support
**Where:** `/tmp/opencode/shadow-t0042/m8_fixsim.json` `stroke_sweep` and `layout_options`
**What I measured.** I thicken the shipped mask by dilation only, no font re-render and no builder code, run it through the proven pipeline (top-left sample to 128 px, then single-step Lanczos to 64), and measure with the same criterion. Stroke 5 tiles reproduces the shipped pattern, simulated row coverage 28.3 for the `I` against 26.7 measured on the real artifact, 28.3 for `O` against 28.3, 46.7 for `E` against 46.7, seven elements still separate. Stroke 6 passes on all seven, ink 87.8 to 128.3 percent of the shipped footprint, ink on 98.3 percent of extent rows for all six letterforms and 88.9 for the heart, the six inter-element gaps still hold, and the enclosed counters survive, `O` 15 tiles wide becoming 13, which is 3.25 px at 64 px. Stroke 7 also passes with counters at 11 tiles. Symmetric thickening at 8 and 10 tiles merges the row into one element, because the gaps are 5 tiles, so uniform thickening is not a route. The budget arithmetic from the measured 48-cell row at 8-tile ground margins, from `m8_fixsim.json`, is that at `S_X` 6 the row has 40 cells and leaves 6 cells, 36 tiles, for the heart. At `S_X` 7 it leaves 0.29 cells and does not fit. At `S_X` 8 `KOREA` alone is 29 cells of the 30 available, so a single row at 8-tile strokes is impossible. Two lines at `S_X` 8 and `S_Y` 16 fit, 15 rows available against 15 needed, letters 112 tiles tall, 28 px at 64 px, strokes 8 tiles, 2 px.
**What it costs, stated so the choice is visible.** At `S_X` 6 the letters stay 238 tiles, 59.5 px at 64 px, which is what the operator's "very big writing" asked for, and the heart becomes 36 tiles wide, about 9 px at 64 px. A filled mass survives that, measured, the shipped heart holds 96.5 percent of its implied ink at 64 px and 96.5 percent even in the 32 px artifact, so the heart is only load-bearing as a mass, never as a stroke. The two-line option keeps a large heart and gets 2 px strokes but halves letter height to 28 or 30 px, a weaker answer to the same instruction, so it ranks second. Fewer characters is not what the numbers point at, because seven elements fit at 6-tile strokes.
**Failure scenario:** a rebuild keeps the single row and simply enlarges the heart, `S_X` stays 5, and the sweep result for stroke 5 repeats, because the heart was never the failing element.

### The editor's viewport-indicator overlay overwrites glyph ink inside the published body crop, and it is carried into both booth artifacts
**Severity:** should-fix
**Where:** `/tmp/opencode/t0042/i-love-korea-mm.png` rows 0 to 11, columns 0 to 26, and everything derived from it, `i-love-korea-booth64.png`, `i-love-korea-booth32.png`, `i-love-korea-mm-2x.png`
**What I measured.** The crop has exactly three colours. The third is pure white, 74 px, forming a solid rectangle 12 rows by 27 columns at the top-left corner of the body, which is SCMDraft's viewport indicator drawn over the map render. I tested those 74 positions against my own mask: **8 of them sit where the map has glyph tiles** and 66 over ground. The visible consequence is on the leftmost letter, the `I`. Its top serif reads 0, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8, 0 glyph px across its eight sampled rows while its bottom serif reads 8 on all eight rows, so the top serif is short at both ends in the crop and in every artifact made from it. The overlay's own contrast against the ground tone is **2.169**, below this task's 3.0 gate, so a below-gate element is present in an artifact recorded as chrome-free. `M7` disclosed the 0.45 percent at doc:2085, which is why this is a should-fix and not a concealment finding. No capture in this set avoids it, `m7-jump.png` puts the indicator at rows 57 to 68, columns 49 to 76, covering 14 mask-glyph positions, worse than the open render.
**Failure scenario:** the booth artifact shows a pale box fused to the top of the `I`, and a reader comparing `i-love-korea-mm.png` against the map finds the serif asymmetric and cannot tell whether the design or the capture caused it. At `S_X` 6 in a rebuild the `I` narrows, so a fixed capture matters before the next artifact set is cut, not after.
**Suggested direction:** take the overview capture with the viewport parked where the indicator lands entirely in the ground margin, and record the indicator rectangle and its map position in the report the way the body rectangle is recorded. If no park position clears the glyph, state in the artifact table that the overlay is part of the published crop and name the px it covers.

### `M7`'s level (a) survival measurement runs a pipeline the published artifact does not take, and on its own it reads all seven letterforms surviving
**Severity:** nit
**Where:** `/tmp/opencode/t0042/i-love-korea_report.json` `survival_4x_mask_lanczos` against `survival_4x_real_booth64`
**What I measured.** Level (a) resamples the shipped mask 256 to 64 in a single Lanczos step and reports all 7 components surviving, `I` at 133.5 percent of expected ink and vertical coverage 1.0. The real artifact is point-sampled 256 to 128 by the editor first, then Lanczos to 64, and on that path the same `I` measures 0.53 of expected ink and 0.267 vertical coverage, which is what my independent census reproduces at 53.0 percent and 26.7 percent. `M7` labelled the three levels separately and used the real path for its conclusion, so nothing is wrong here, but the record is quotable in pieces.
**Failure scenario:** a later dispatch, `S1` or `V1`, quotes `components_after_4x_8conn = 7` from level (a) as evidence that a 5-tile stroke survives 4x, designs the next map on it, and the real artifact fails the way this one did.
**Suggested direction:** one sentence beside each level naming the pipeline it measures, mask-only Lanczos versus editor-sample-then-Lanczos, and keep the real-path figures as the ones a gate decision is taken from.

### Two `## Implementation` headings in one doc, nit for the record rather than for the maps
**Severity:** nit
**Where:** doc:482 and doc:1605, both `## Implementation`, with `## Review` at doc:2104 and `## Security` now following this entry
**Problem.** The fixed section order at the head of this doc is binding and one section title appears twice. I did not touch it, this read is findings only.
**Failure scenario:** `Espio` prunes with a section-scoped tool and either stops at the first boundary or sweeps both blocks into `## Archive`, taking shipped dispatch records with them.
**Suggested direction:** `Espio` or `Robotnik` resolves the duplicate, it is their structural call, not a reviewer's.

**Agreements I measured rather than inherited.** Coverage 30.01 percent and the 6.745 pair are mine from the shipped file and they match. The body crop is real, I located the 128x128 map render independently by a middle-row signature search rather than by clustering, it lands at (18,112) to (146,240), byte-identical in `m7-open.png` and `m7-reopen.png`, 150 px different in `m7-jump.png` and only there, which is the indicator moving. The pixels just outside that window are [245,245,245] and [166,166,166] panel chrome, so the window is bounded by chrome and is the map body, and its measured dark extent, rows 5 to 123 and columns 4 to 123, matches the extent my mask predicts at 2 tiles per px, rows 4.5 to 123 and columns 4 to 123.5. `booth64` and `booth32` are exact single-step Lanczos at 0.5 and 0.25 on both axes of the crop I located, 0 px maximum difference over 4096 and 1024 px, while a nearest-neighbour resize of the same crop differs by up to 122 and 99, so the uniform-scale claim is confirmed and no stretch is possible. Re-open is identical by my own crop-to-crop diff, 0 px over 16384, and a booth64 made from the re-open crop is identical to the published booth64. Controller hashes I re-ran myself, `i-love-korea` `82589b4205f5deb8...`, and the five earlier deliverables unchanged at `e856f0e1d62e`, `447360ce8077`, `b9b056c27a67`, `79002eacd5d6`, `c773a534a564`. `F1`'s rename is confirmed on disk, the plain name `foss-for-all.scm` now holds the accepted `e856f0e1...` Ice build, so my earlier finding at doc:2707 is closed and `S1` can build open-file steps from the plain names. The title bar in both of my own crops, `m7-open` and `m7-reopen`, reads `SCmdDraft 2.0 (Beta 0.9.10) - [/home/howard/scmdraft-maps/i-love-korea/i-love-korea.scm ]`, the fourth capture set in this task showing the path, and the status bar reads `Ice World\Snow`.

**Verdict on the gate question this dispatch was staged to answer.** At the real whole-map overview render, 128 px, **the row reads**, my own numbers are 88.6 to 100.3 percent of implied ink on every element with INK on 100 percent of each element's extent rows, and my visual read at that size resolves `I`, a heart, and five separable letterforms, which is the reading a booth audience gets from the editor panel. At the certified 64 px bound, **the row does not read as shipped**, `I`, `O` and `E` hold 53.0, 29.4 and 60.7 percent of their own implied ink and 26.7, 28.3 and 46.7 percent of their height, four of seven elements pass and three do not. 32 px is out of scope on the same bound already recorded for `tux` and for `republic-of-korea`, and my numbers there, only the heart passing at 15.72 percent ink overall, are consistent with it. `i-love-korea` is therefore **not booth-accepted as shipped, and it is not blocked**, the failure is a stroke-width choice and the measurement names the value that clears it. The fix the numbers point at is the single row at `S_X` 6 with the heart capped at 6 cells, 36 tiles, because at 6 tiles all seven elements pass my criterion through the proven editor pipeline while the counters and the six gaps survive and letter height stays at 238 tiles, which is what "very big" means in this design. Two lines at `S_X` 8 and `S_Y` 16 is the fallback, it also passes and it keeps a big heart, but it halves letter height to 112 tiles and answers the operator's instruction less well. Dropping characters is not indicated, seven elements fit at 6-tile strokes. `S1` must not list `i-love-korea` as an accepted map until a rebuilt map is measured at the real 64 px path, and the rebuild is a re-parameterisation of the existing pipeline rather than a redesign, so it costs one build dispatch, and the four gate numbers themselves, coverage, the 6.745 pair, uniform chrome-light booth artifacts, identical re-open, all pass on this build. The control limit travels with this verdict, as it did through `M4` and `M6`. I knew the intended row before I looked, so this read proves that the letterforms carry or do not carry, and it does not prove that a reader who has never opened this doc would supply the word.

**Checks that did not run, named.** No target contact, so the placed copy at `~/scmdraft-maps/i-love-korea/i-love-korea.scm` on 192.168.1.99, its size, mtime and SHA-256, is not verified by this read, my hashes are on the controller copies. SCMDraft 2's own map-validation dialog, still run on no map in this task, `V1` remains the only dispatch that will run it. Any literal in-game StarCraft render or in-game minimap, so the DoD minimap box stays met only by the editor-thumbnail proxy. Tile elevation class of values 64 and 192 on era 6. Physical booth legibility at a real panel at a real distance, the booth display size is still recorded nowhere in this task. Decoding of `MRGN `, 1280 B present and unread. A rebuild at `S_X` 6, my 6-tile result is a dilation of the shipped mask run through the proven sampling and Lanczos path, validated against the shipped artifact at stroke 5 and not against a real rebuild capture, so the rebuild's own numbers are unmeasured until someone builds it. Whether `rocky.py` `_heart` will still read as a heart at 6 cells wide is my inference from mass-class survival, not a measurement. Whether the font's `I` at `S_X` 6 keeps its 10-tile floor on both axes, the serif bars become 18 tiles and the stem 6, so the floor holds on the letterform unit but the 4-connected fragments become 6 tiles and still sit under 10, which is the same condition `M7` recorded at doc:2076 and I did not re-adjudicate. OCR of `m7-read-status-3x.png`, I read the status bar text from the capture directly. Visual reading of `i-love-korea-mm-2x.png`, `m7-read-body-3x.png` and `m7-read-booth64.png`, which I measured or listed and did not read as images, and my own upscales `m8-booth64-4x.png`, `m8-crop128-2x.png` and `m8-booth32-8x.png` are nearest-neighbour viewing aids that no measurement uses. A blind identifiability test with a reader who has not read this doc. Execution of any builder script, `build_ts.py`, `glyph.py`, `rocky.py`, `m7_measure.py`.
